GeForce Experience enhances each gamer's experience by optimizing their PC's settings, as well as enabling the recording and sharing of gameplay.

[nvda_2024_1_P036_S008] Parent nvda_2024_1_P036 - Item 1 - Business Strategies
Our Studio drivers enhance and accelerate a number of popular creative applications.

[nvda_2024_1_P036_S009] Parent nvda_2024_1_P036 - Item 1 - Business Strategies
Omniverse is real-time 3D design collaboration and virtual world simulation software that empowers artists, designers, and creators to connect and collaborate in leading design applications.

[nvda_2024_1_P036_S010] Parent nvda_2024_1_P036 - Item 1 - Business Strategies
We also enable interactive graphics applications - such as games, movie and photo editing and design software - to be accessed by almost any device, almost anywhere, through our cloud platforms such as vGPU for enterprise and GeForce NOW for gaming.

[nvda_2024_1_P037_S001] Parent nvda_2024_1_P037 - Item 1 - Business Strategies
Advancing the leading autonomous vehicle platform.

[nvda_2024_1_P037_S002] Parent nvda_2024_1_P037 - Item 1 - Business Strategies
We believe the advent of autonomous vehicles, or AV, and electric vehicles, or EV, is revolutionizing the transportation industry.

[nvda_2024_1_P037_S003] Parent nvda_2024_1_P037 - Item 1 - Business Strategies
The algorithms required for autonomous driving - such as perception, localization, and planning - are too complex for legacy hand-coded approaches and will use multiple neural networks instead.

[nvda_2024_1_P037_S004] Parent nvda_2024_1_P037 - Item 1 - Business Strategies
In addition, EV makers are looking for next-generation centralized car computers that integrate a wide range of intelligent functions into a single AI compute platform.

[nvda_2024_1_P037_S005] Parent nvda_2024_1_P037 - Item 1 - Business Strategies
Therefore, we provide an AI-based hardware and software solution, designed and implemented from the ground up based on automotive safety standards, for the AV and EV market under the DRIVE brand, which we are bringing to market through our partnerships with automotive OEMs, tier-1 suppliers, and start-ups.

[nvda_2024_1_P037_S006] Parent nvda_2024_1_P037 - Item 1 - Business Strategies
Our AV solution also includes the GPU-based hardware required to train the neural networks before their in-vehicle deployment, as well as to re-simulate their operation prior to any over-the-air software updates.

[nvda_2024_1_P037_S007] Parent nvda_2024_1_P037 - Item 1 - Business Strategies
We believe our comprehensive, top-to-bottom and end-to-end approach will enable the transportation industry to solve the complex problems arising from the shift to autonomous driving.

[nvda_2024_1_P043_S001] Parent nvda_2024_1_P043 - Item 1 - Manufacturing

[nvda_2024_1_P043_S002] Parent nvda_2024_1_P043 - Item 1 - Manufacturing

[nvda_2024_1_P043_S004] Parent nvda_2024_1_P043 - Item 1 - Manufacturing

[nvda_2024_1_P043_S005] Parent nvda_2024_1_P043 - Item 1 - Manufacturing

[nvda_2024_1_P043_S006] Parent nvda_2024_1_P043 - Item 1 - Manufacturing

[nvda_2024_1_P044_S001] Parent nvda_2024_1_P044 - Item 1 - Manufacturing

[nvda_2024_1_P050_S001] Parent nvda_2024_1_P050 - Item 1 - Government Regulations
Our worldwide business activities are subject to various laws, rules, and regulations of the United States as well as of foreign governments.

[nvda_2024_1_P051_S001] Parent nvda_2024_1_P051 - Item 1 - Government Regulations
During the third quarter of fiscal year 2023, the U.S. government, or the USG, announced licensing requirements that, with certain exceptions, impact exports to China (including Hong Kong and Macau) and Russia of our A100 and H100 integrated circuits, DGX or any other systems or boards which incorporate A100 or H100 integrated circuits.

[nvda_2024_1_P052_S001] Parent nvda_2024_1_P052 - Item 1 - Government Regulations
In July 2023, the USG informed us of an additional licensing requirement for a subset of A100 and H100 products destined to certain customers and other regions, including some countries in the Middle East.

[nvda_2024_1_P053_S001] Parent nvda_2024_1_P053 - Item 1 - Government Regulations
In October 2023, the USG announced new and updated licensing requirements that became effective in our fourth quarter of fiscal year 2024 for exports to China and Country Groups D1, D4, and D5 (including but not limited to Saudi Arabia, the United Arab Emirates, and Vietnam, but excluding Israel) of our products exceeding certain performance thresholds, including A100, A800, H100, H800, L4, L40, L40S and RTX 4090.

[nvda_2024_1_P053_S002] Parent nvda_2024_1_P053 - Item 1 - Government Regulations
The licensing requirements also apply to the export of products exceeding certain performance thresholds to a party headquartered in, or with an ultimate parent headquartered in, Country Group D5, including China.

[nvda_2024_1_P053_S003] Parent nvda_2024_1_P053 - Item 1 - Government Regulations
On October 23, 2023, the USG informed us the licensing requirements were effective immediately for shipments of our A100, A800, H100, H800, and L40S products.

[nvda_2024_1_P054_S001] Parent nvda_2024_1_P054 - Item 1 - Government Regulations
Our competitive position has been harmed, and our competitive position and future results may be further harmed in the long term, if there are further changes in the USG's export controls.

[nvda_2024_1_P054_S002] Parent nvda_2024_1_P054 - Item 1 - Government Regulations
Given the increasing strategic importance of AI and rising geopolitical tensions, the USG has changed and may again change the export control rules at any time and further subject a wider range of our products to export restrictions and licensing requirements, negatively impacting our business and financial results.

[nvda_2024_1_P054_S003] Parent nvda_2024_1_P054 - Item 1 - Government Regulations
In the event of such change, we may be unable to sell our inventory of such products and may be unable to develop replacement products not subject to the licensing requirements, effectively excluding us from all or part of the China market, as well as other impacted markets, including the Middle East.

[nvda_2024_1_P055_S001] Parent nvda_2024_1_P055 - Item 1 - Government Regulations
While we work to enhance the resiliency and redundancy of our supply chain, which is currently concentrated in the Asia-Pacific region, new and existing export controls or changes to existing export controls could limit alternative manufacturing locations and negatively impact our business.

[nvda_2024_1_P055_S002] Parent nvda_2024_1_P055 - Item 1 - Government Regulations
Refer to "Item 1A. Risk Factors - Risks Related to Regulatory, Legal, Our Stock and Other Matters" for a discussion of this potential impact.

[nvda_2024_1_P056_S001] Parent nvda_2024_1_P056 - Item 1 - Government Regulations
Compliance with laws, rules, and regulations has not otherwise had a material effect upon our capital expenditures, results of operations, or competitive position and we do not currently anticipate material capital expenditures for environmental control facilities.

[nvda_2024_1_P056_S002] Parent nvda_2024_1_P056 - Item 1 - Government Regulations

These competitors may be able to acquire market share and/or prevent us from doing so, more effectively identify and capitalize upon opportunities in new markets and end-user trends, more quickly transition their products, and impinge on our ability to procure sufficient foundry capacity and scarce input materials during a supply-constrained environment, which could harm our business.

[nvda_2024_1A_P012_S005] Parent nvda_2024_1A_P012 - Item 1A - Risks Related to Our Industry and Markets

[nvda_2024_1A_P012_S006] Parent nvda_2024_1A_P012 - Item 1A - Risks Related to Our Industry and Markets

[nvda_2024_1A_P108_S003] Parent nvda_2024_1A_P108 - Item 1A - Risks Related to Regulatory, Legal, Our Stock and Other Matters
These provisions include the ability of our Board of Directors to create and issue preferred stock, change the number of directors, and to make, amend or repeal our bylaws without prior shareholder approval; the inability of our shareholders to act by written consent or call special meetings; advance notice requirements for director nominations and shareholder proposals; and a super-majority voting requirement to amend some provisions in our certificate of incorporation and bylaws.

[nvda_2024_1A_P108_S004] Parent nvda_2024_1A_P108 - Item 1A - Risks Related to Regulatory, Legal, Our Stock and Other Matters
Under our agreement with Microsoft for the Xbox, if someone makes an offer to purchase at least 30% of our outstanding common stock, Microsoft may have first and last rights of refusal to purchase the stock.

[nvda_2024_1A_P108_S005] Parent nvda_2024_1A_P108 - Item 1A - Risks Related to Regulatory, Legal, Our Stock and Other Matters
These provisions could delay or prevent a change in control of NVIDIA, discourage proxy contests, and make it more difficult for shareholders to elect directors of their choosing and to cause us to take other corporate actions they desire.

[nvda_2024_7_P001_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P001 - Item 7 - Management's Discussion and Analysis
The following discussion and analysis of our financial condition and results of operations should be read in conjunction with "Item 1A. Risk Factors", our Consolidated Financial Statements and related Notes thereto, as well as other cautionary statements and risks described elsewhere in this Annual Report on Form 10-K, before deciding to purchase, hold or sell shares of our common stock.

[nvda_2024_7_P002_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P002 - Item 7 - Our Company and Our Businesses
NVIDIA pioneered accelerated computing to help solve the most challenging computational problems.

[nvda_2024_7_P002_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P002 - Item 7 - Our Company and Our Businesses
Since our original focus on PC graphics, we have expanded to several other large and important computationally intensive fields.

[nvda_2024_7_P002_S003] Parent nvda_2024_7_P002 - Item 7 - Our Company and Our Businesses
NVIDIA has leveraged its GPU architecture to create platforms for accelerated computing, AI solutions, scientific computing, data science, AV, robotics, metaverse and 3D internet applications.

[nvda_2024_7_P003_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P003 - Item 7 - Our Company and Our Businesses

[nvda_2024_7_P004_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P004 - Item 7 - Our Company and Our Businesses
Headquartered in Santa Clara, California, NVIDIA was incorporated in California in April 1993 and reincorporated in Delaware in April 1998.

[nvda_2024_7_P005_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P005 - Item 7 - Demand and Supply, Product Transitions, and New Products and Business Models
Demand for our data center systems and products surged in fiscal year 2024.

[nvda_2024_7_P005_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P005 - Item 7 - Demand and Supply, Product Transitions, and New Products and Business Models

[nvda_2024_7_P005_S003] Parent nvda_2024_7_P005 - Item 7 - Demand and Supply, Product Transitions, and New Products and Business Models
We have demand visibility for our new data center products ramping later in fiscal year 2025.

[nvda_2024_7_P005_S004] Parent nvda_2024_7_P005 - Item 7 - Demand and Supply, Product Transitions, and New Products and Business Models
We have increased our supply and capacity purchases with existing suppliers, added new vendors and entered into prepaid manufacturing and capacity agreements.

[nvda_2024_7_P005_S005] Parent nvda_2024_7_P005 - Item 7 - Demand and Supply, Product Transitions, and New Products and Business Models

[nvda_2024_7_P005_S006] Parent nvda_2024_7_P005 - Item 7 - Demand and Supply, Product Transitions, and New Products and Business Models
Our purchase commitments and obligations for inventory and manufacturing capacity at the end of fiscal year 2024 were impacted by shortening lead times for certain components.

[nvda_2024_7_P005_S007] Parent nvda_2024_7_P005 - Item 7 - Demand and Supply, Product Transitions, and New Products and Business Models

[nvda_2024_7_P005_S008] Parent nvda_2024_7_P005 - Item 7 - Demand and Supply, Product Transitions, and New Products and Business Models
Supply of Hopper architecture products is improving, and demand remains very strong.

[nvda_2024_7_P005_S009] Parent nvda_2024_7_P005 - Item 7 - Demand and Supply, Product Transitions, and New Products and Business Models
We expect our next-generation products to be supply-constrained based upon demand indications.

[nvda_2024_7_P005_S010] Parent nvda_2024_7_P005 - Item 7 - Demand and Supply, Product Transitions, and New Products and Business Models

[nvda_2024_7_P006_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P006 - Item 7 - Demand and Supply, Product Transitions, and New Products and Business Models
We build finished products and maintain inventory in advance of anticipated demand.

[nvda_2024_7_P006_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P006 - Item 7 - Demand and Supply, Product Transitions, and New Products and Business Models
While we have entered into long-term supply and capacity commitments, we may not be able to secure sufficient commitments for capacity to address our business needs, or our long-term demand expectations may change.

[nvda_2024_7_P006_S003] Parent nvda_2024_7_P006 - Item 7 - Demand and Supply, Product Transitions, and New Products and Business Models
These risks may increase as we shorten our product development cycles, enter new lines of business, or integrate new suppliers or components into our supply chain, creating additional supply chain complexity.

[nvda_2024_7_P007_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P007 - Item 7 - Demand and Supply, Product Transitions, and New Products and Business Models
Product transitions are complex as we often ship both new and prior architecture products simultaneously and we and our channel partners prepare to ship and support new products.

[nvda_2024_7_P007_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P007 - Item 7 - Demand and Supply, Product Transitions, and New Products and Business Models
Due to our product introduction cycles, we are almost always in various stages of transitioning the architecture of our Data Center, Professional Visualization, and Gaming products.

[nvda_2024_7_P007_S003] Parent nvda_2024_7_P007 - Item 7 - Demand and Supply, Product Transitions, and New Products and Business Models
We will have a broader and faster Data Center product launch cadence to meet a growing and diverse set of AI opportunities.

[nvda_2024_7_P007_S004] Parent nvda_2024_7_P007 - Item 7 - Demand and Supply, Product Transitions, and New Products and Business Models
The increased frequency of these transitions may magnify the challenges associated with managing our supply and demand due to manufacturing lead times.

[nvda_2024_7_P007_S005] Parent nvda_2024_7_P007 - Item 7 - Demand and Supply, Product Transitions, and New Products and Business Models
Qualification time for new products, customers anticipating product transitions and channel partners reducing channel inventory of prior architectures ahead of new product introductions can create reductions or volatility in our revenue.

[nvda_2024_7_P007_S006] Parent nvda_2024_7_P007 - Item 7 - Demand and Supply, Product Transitions, and New Products and Business Models
The increasing frequency and complexity of newly introduced products could result in quality or production issues that could increase inventory provisions, warranty or other costs or result in product delays.

[nvda_2024_7_P007_S007] Parent nvda_2024_7_P007 - Item 7 - Demand and Supply, Product Transitions, and New Products and Business Models
Deployment of new products to customers creates additional challenges due to the complexity of our technologies, which has impacted and may in the future impact the timing of customer purchases or otherwise impact our demand.

[nvda_2024_7_P007_S008] Parent nvda_2024_7_P007 - Item 7 - Demand and Supply, Product Transitions, and New Products and Business Models
While we have managed prior product transitions and have previously sold multiple product architectures at the same time, these transitions are difficult, may impair our ability to predict demand and impact our supply mix, and we may incur additional costs.

[nvda_2024_7_P008_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P008 - Item 7 - Demand and Supply, Product Transitions, and New Products and Business Models
We build technology and introduce products for new and innovative use cases and applications such as our NVIDIA DGX Cloud services, Omniverse platform, LLMs, and generative AI models.

[nvda_2024_7_P008_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P008 - Item 7 - Demand and Supply, Product Transitions, and New Products and Business Models

[nvda_2024_7_P008_S003] Parent nvda_2024_7_P008 - Item 7 - Demand and Supply, Product Transitions, and New Products and Business Models
Recent technologies, such as generative AI models, have emerged, and while they have driven increased demand for Data Center, the long-term trajectory is unknown.

[nvda_2024_7_P009_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P009 - Item 7 - Global Trade
During the third quarter of fiscal year 2023, the USG, announced licensing requirements that, with certain exceptions, impact exports to China (including Hong Kong and Macau) and Russia of our A100 and H100 integrated circuits, DGX or any other systems or boards which incorporate A100 or H100 integrated circuits.

[nvda_2024_7_P010_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P010 - Item 7 - Global Trade
In July 2023, the USG informed us of an additional licensing requirement for a subset of A100 and H100 products destined to certain customers and other regions, including some countries in the Middle East.

[nvda_2024_7_P011_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P011 - Item 7 - Global Trade
In October 2023, the USG announced new and updated licensing requirements that became effective in our fourth quarter of fiscal year 2024 for exports to China and Country Groups D1, D4, and D5 (including but not limited to Saudi Arabia, the United Arab Emirates, and Vietnam, but excluding Israel) of our products exceeding certain performance thresholds, including A100, A800, H100, H800, L4, L40, L40S and RTX 4090.

[nvda_2024_7_P011_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P011 - Item 7 - Global Trade
The licensing requirements also apply to the export of products exceeding certain performance thresholds to a party headquartered in, or with an ultimate parent headquartered in, Country Group D5, including China.

[nvda_2024_7_P011_S003] Parent nvda_2024_7_P011 - Item 7 - Global Trade
On October 23, 2023, the USG informed us the licensing requirements were effective immediately for shipments of our A100, A800, H100, H800, and L40S products.

[nvda_2024_7_P011_S004] Parent nvda_2024_7_P011 - Item 7 - Global Trade
Our sales to China decreased as a percentage of total Data Center revenue from 19% in fiscal year 2023 to 14% in fiscal year 2024.

[nvda_2024_7_P012_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P012 - Item 7 - Global Trade
We have not received licenses to ship these restricted products to China.

[nvda_2024_7_P012_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P012 - Item 7 - Global Trade
We are working to expand our Data Center product portfolio to offer new solutions, including those for which the USG does not require a license or advance notice before each shipment.

[nvda_2024_7_P012_S003] Parent nvda_2024_7_P012 - Item 7 - Global Trade
We have started to ship alternatives to the China market in small volumes.

[nvda_2024_7_P012_S004] Parent nvda_2024_7_P012 - Item 7 - Global Trade
China represented a mid-single digit percentage of our Data Center revenue in the fourth quarter of fiscal year 2024 due to USG licensing requirements and we expect China to be in a similar range in the first quarter of fiscal year 2025.

[nvda_2024_7_P012_S005] Parent nvda_2024_7_P012 - Item 7 - Global Trade
To the extent that a customer requires products covered by the licensing requirements, we may seek a license for the customer but have no assurance that the USG will grant such a license, or that the USG will act on the license application in a timely manner or at all.

[nvda_2024_7_P013_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P013 - Item 7 - Global Trade
Our competitive position has been harmed, and our competitive position and future results may be further harmed in the long term, if there are further changes in the USG's export controls.

[nvda_2024_7_P013_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P013 - Item 7 - Global Trade
Given the increasing strategic importance of AI and rising geopolitical tensions, the USG has changed and may again change the export control rules at any time and further subject a wider range of our products to export restrictions and licensing requirements, negatively impacting our business and financial results.

[nvda_2024_7_P013_S003] Parent nvda_2024_7_P013 - Item 7 - Global Trade
In the event of such change, we may be unable to sell our inventory of such products and may be unable to develop replacement products not subject to the licensing requirements, effectively excluding us from all or part of the China market, as well as other impacted markets, including the Middle East.

[nvda_2024_7_P014_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P014 - Item 7 - Global Trade
While we work to enhance the resiliency and redundancy of our supply chain, which is currently concentrated in the Asia-Pacific region, new and existing export controls or changes to existing export controls could limit alternative manufacturing locations and negatively impact our business.

[nvda_2024_7_P014_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P014 - Item 7 - Global Trade
Refer to "Item 1A. Risk Factors - Risks Related to Regulatory, Legal, Our Stock and Other Matters" for a discussion of this potential impact.

[nvda_2024_7_P015_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P015 - Item 7 - Macroeconomic Factors
Macroeconomic factors, including inflation, increased interest rates, capital market volatility, global supply chain constraints and global economic and geopolitical developments, may have direct and indirect impacts on our results of operations, particularly demand for our products.

[nvda_2024_7_P015_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P015 - Item 7 - Macroeconomic Factors
While difficult to isolate and quantify, these macroeconomic factors can also impact our supply chain and manufacturing costs, employee wages, costs for capital equipment and value of our investments.

[nvda_2024_7_P015_S003] Parent nvda_2024_7_P015 - Item 7 - Macroeconomic Factors
Our product and solution pricing generally does not fluctuate with short-term changes in our costs.

[nvda_2024_7_P015_S004] Parent nvda_2024_7_P015 - Item 7 - Macroeconomic Factors
Within our supply chain, we continuously manage product availability and costs with our vendors.

[nvda_2024_7_P016_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P016 - Item 7 - Israel and Hamas Conflict
We are monitoring the impact of the geopolitical conflict in and around Israel on our operations, including the health and safety of our approximately 3,700 employees in the region who primarily support the research and development, operations, and sales and marketing of our networking products.

[nvda_2024_7_P016_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P016 - Item 7 - Israel and Hamas Conflict
Our operating expenses in fiscal year 2024 include expenses for financial support to impacted employees and charitable activity.

[nvda_2024_7_P016_S003] Parent nvda_2024_7_P016 - Item 7 - Israel and Hamas Conflict

[nvda_2024_7_P016_S004] Parent nvda_2024_7_P016 - Item 7 - Israel and Hamas Conflict
Further, in connection with the conflict, a substantial number of our employees in the region have been called-up for active military duty in Israel.

[nvda_2024_7_P016_S005] Parent nvda_2024_7_P016 - Item 7 - Israel and Hamas Conflict
Accordingly, some of our employees in Israel have been absent for an extended period and they or others may continue to be absent, which may cause disruption to our product development or operations.

[nvda_2024_7_P016_S006] Parent nvda_2024_7_P016 - Item 7 - Israel and Hamas Conflict
We did not experience any significant impact or expense to our business; however, if the conflict is further extended, it could impact future product development, operations, and revenue or create other uncertainty for our business.

[nvda_2024_7_P025_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P025 - Item 7 - Market Platform Highlights
Data Center revenue for fiscal year 2024 was $47.5 billion, up 217% from fiscal year 2023.

[nvda_2024_7_P025_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P025 - Item 7 - Market Platform Highlights
In Data Center, we launched AI inference platforms that combine our full-stack inference software with NVIDIA Ada, NVIDIA Hopper and NVIDIA Grace Hopper processors optimized for generative AI, LLMs and other AI workloads.

[nvda_2024_7_P025_S003] Parent nvda_2024_7_P025 - Item 7 - Market Platform Highlights
We introduced NVIDIA DGX Cloud and AI Foundations to help businesses create and operate custom large language models and generative AI models.

[nvda_2024_7_P025_S004] Parent nvda_2024_7_P025 - Item 7 - Market Platform Highlights
As AV algorithms move to video transformers, and more cars are equipped with cameras, we expect NVIDIA's automotive data center processing demand to grow significantly.

[nvda_2024_7_P025_S005] Parent nvda_2024_7_P025 - Item 7 - Market Platform Highlights
We estimate that in fiscal year 2024, approximately 40% of Data Center revenue was for AI inference.

[nvda_2024_7_P025_S006] Parent nvda_2024_7_P025 - Item 7 - Market Platform Highlights

[nvda_2024_7_P026_S003] Parent nvda_2024_7_P026 - Item 7 - Market Platform Highlights

[nvda_2024_7_P026_S005] Parent nvda_2024_7_P026 - Item 7 - Market Platform Highlights

[nvda_2024_7_P027_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P027 - Item 7 - Market Platform Highlights

[nvda_2024_7_P028_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P028 - Item 7 - Market Platform Highlights

[nvda_2024_7_P028_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P028 - Item 7 - Market Platform Highlights

[nvda_2024_7_P028_S003] Parent nvda_2024_7_P028 - Item 7 - Market Platform Highlights

[nvda_2024_7_P029_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P029 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
Our consolidated financial statements are prepared in accordance with accounting principles generally accepted in the United States, or U.S. GAAP.

[nvda_2024_7_P029_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P029 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
The preparation of these financial statements requires us to make estimates and judgments that affect the reported amounts of assets, liabilities, revenue, cost of revenue, expenses and related disclosure of contingencies.

[nvda_2024_7_P029_S003] Parent nvda_2024_7_P029 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
Critical accounting estimates are those estimates that involve a significant level of estimation uncertainty and could have a material impact on our financial condition or results of operations.

[nvda_2024_7_P029_S004] Parent nvda_2024_7_P029 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
We have critical accounting estimates in the areas of inventories, revenue recognition, and income taxes.

[nvda_2024_7_P029_S005] Parent nvda_2024_7_P029 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
Refer to Note 1 of the Notes to the Consolidated Financial Statements in Part IV, Item 15 of this Annual Report on Form 10-K for a summary of significant accounting policies.

[nvda_2024_7_P030_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P030 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Inventories
We charge cost of sales for inventory provisions to write-down our inventory to the lower of cost or net realizable value or for obsolete or excess inventory, and for excess product purchase commitments.

[nvda_2024_7_P030_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P030 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Inventories
Most of our inventory provisions relate to excess quantities of products or components, based on our inventory levels and future product purchase commitments compared to assumptions about future demand and market conditions, which requires management judgment.

[nvda_2024_7_P031_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P031 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Inventories
Situations that may result in excess or obsolete inventory or excess product purchase commitments include changes in business and economic conditions, changes in market conditions, sudden and significant decreases in demand for our products, inventory obsolescence because of changing technology and customer requirements, new product introductions resulting in less demand for existing products or inconsistent spikes in demand, failure to estimate customer demand properly, ordering in advance of historical lead-times, government regulations and the impact of changes in future demand, or increase in demand for competitive products, including competitive actions.

[nvda_2024_7_P031_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P031 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Inventories
Cancellation or deferral of customer purchase orders could result in our holding excess inventory.

[nvda_2024_7_P032_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P032 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Inventories
The net effect on our gross margin from inventory provisions and sales of items previously written down was an unfavorable impact of 2.7% in fiscal year 2024 and 7.5% in fiscal year 2023.

[nvda_2024_7_P032_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P032 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Inventories
Our inventory and capacity purchase commitments are based on forecasts of future customer demand.

[nvda_2024_7_P032_S003] Parent nvda_2024_7_P032 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Inventories
We account for our third-party manufacturers' lead times and constraints.

[nvda_2024_7_P032_S004] Parent nvda_2024_7_P032 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Inventories
Our manufacturing lead times can be and have been long, and in some cases, extended beyond twelve months for some products.

[nvda_2024_7_P032_S005] Parent nvda_2024_7_P032 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Inventories
We may place non-cancellable inventory orders for certain product components in advance of our historical lead times, pay premiums and provide deposits to secure future supply and capacity.

[nvda_2024_7_P032_S006] Parent nvda_2024_7_P032 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Inventories
We also adjust to other market factors, such as product offerings and pricing actions by our competitors, new product transitions, and macroeconomic conditions - all of which may impact demand for our products.

[nvda_2024_7_P033_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P033 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Inventories
Refer to the Gross Profit and Gross Margin discussion below in this Management's Discussion and Analysis for further discussion.

[nvda_2024_7_P034_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P034 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Income Taxes
We are subject to income taxes in the U.S. and foreign jurisdictions.

[nvda_2024_7_P034_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P034 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Income Taxes
Our calculation of deferred tax assets and liabilities is based on certain estimates and judgments and involves dealing with uncertainties in the application of complex tax laws.

[nvda_2024_7_P034_S003] Parent nvda_2024_7_P034 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Income Taxes
Our estimates of deferred tax assets and liabilities may change based, in part, on added certainty or finality to an anticipated outcome, changes in accounting standards or tax laws in the U.S. or foreign jurisdictions where we operate, or changes in other facts or circumstances.

[nvda_2024_7_P034_S004] Parent nvda_2024_7_P034 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Income Taxes
In addition, we recognize liabilities for potential U.S. and foreign income tax contingencies based on our estimate of whether, and the extent to which, additional taxes may be due.

[nvda_2024_7_P034_S005] Parent nvda_2024_7_P034 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Income Taxes
If we determine that payment of these amounts is unnecessary or if the recorded tax liability is less than our current assessment, we may be required to recognize an income tax benefit or additional income tax expense in our financial statements accordingly.

[nvda_2024_7_P035_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P035 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Income Taxes
As of the end of fiscal years 2024 and 2023, we had a valuation allowance of $1.6 billion and $1.5 billion, respectively, related to capital loss carryforwards, and certain state and other deferred tax assets that management determined are not likely to be realized due, in part, to jurisdictional projections of future taxable income, including capital gains.

[nvda_2024_7_P035_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P035 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Income Taxes
To the extent realization of the deferred tax assets becomes more-likely-than-not, we would recognize such deferred tax assets as income tax benefits during the period.

[nvda_2024_7_P036_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P036 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Income Taxes
We recognize the benefit from a tax position only if it is more-likely-than-not that the position would be sustained upon audit based solely on the technical merits of the tax position.

[nvda_2024_7_P036_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P036 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Income Taxes
Our policy is to include interest and penalties related to unrecognized tax benefits as a component of income tax expense.

[nvda_2024_7_P037_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P037 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Revenue Recognition


[nvda_2024_7_P038_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P038 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Revenue Allowances
For products sold with a right of return, we record a reduction to revenue by establishing a sales return allowance for estimated product returns at the time revenue is recognized, based primarily on historical return rates.

[nvda_2024_7_P038_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P038 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Revenue Allowances
However, if product returns for a fiscal period are anticipated to exceed historical return rates, we may determine that additional sales return allowances are required to reflect our estimated exposure for product returns.

[nvda_2024_7_P038_S003] Parent nvda_2024_7_P038 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Revenue Allowances
Return rights for certain stocking distributors for specific products are contractually limited based on a percentage of prior quarter shipments.

[nvda_2024_7_P038_S004] Parent nvda_2024_7_P038 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Revenue Allowances
For shipments to other customers, we do not allow returns, although we may approve returns for credit or refund based on applicable facts and circumstances.

[nvda_2024_7_P039_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P039 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Revenue Allowances
We account for customer programs, which involve rebates and marketing development funds, as a reduction in revenue and accrue for such programs based on the amount we expect to be claimed by customers.

[nvda_2024_7_P039_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P039 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Revenue Allowances
Certain customer programs include distributor price incentives or other channel programs for specific products and customer classes which require judgement as to whether the applicable incentives will be attained.

[nvda_2024_7_P039_S003] Parent nvda_2024_7_P039 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Revenue Allowances
Estimates for customer program accruals include a combination of historical attainment and claim rates and may be adjusted based on relevant internal and external factors.

[nvda_2024_7_P040_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P040 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > License and Development Arrangements
Revenue from License and Development Arrangements is recognized over the period in which the development services are performed.

[nvda_2024_7_P040_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P040 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > License and Development Arrangements
Each fiscal reporting period, we measure progress to completion based on actual cost incurred to date as a percentage of the estimated total cost required to complete each project.

[nvda_2024_7_P040_S003] Parent nvda_2024_7_P040 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > License and Development Arrangements
Estimated total cost for each project includes a forecast of internal engineer personnel time expected to be incurred and other third-party costs as applicable.

[nvda_2024_7_P041_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P041 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Contracts with Multiple Performance Obligations
Our contracts may contain more than one performance obligation.

[nvda_2024_7_P041_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P041 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Contracts with Multiple Performance Obligations
Judgement is required in determining whether each performance obligation within a customer contract is distinct.

[nvda_2024_7_P041_S003] Parent nvda_2024_7_P041 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Contracts with Multiple Performance Obligations
Except for License and Development Arrangements, NVIDIA products and services function on a standalone basis and do not require a significant amount of integration or interdependency.

[nvda_2024_7_P041_S004] Parent nvda_2024_7_P041 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Contracts with Multiple Performance Obligations
Therefore, multiple performance obligations contained within a customer contract are considered distinct and are not combined for revenue recognition purposes.

[nvda_2024_7_P042_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P042 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Contracts with Multiple Performance Obligations
We allocate the total transaction price to each distinct performance obligation in a multiple performance obligations arrangement on a relative standalone selling price basis.

[nvda_2024_7_P042_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P042 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Contracts with Multiple Performance Obligations
In certain cases, we can establish standalone selling price based on directly observable prices of products or services sold separately in comparable circumstances to similar customers.

[nvda_2024_7_P042_S003] Parent nvda_2024_7_P042 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Contracts with Multiple Performance Obligations
If standalone selling price is not directly observable, such as when we do not sell a product or service separately, we determine standalone selling price based on market data and other observable inputs.

[nvda_2024_7_P043_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P043 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Change in Accounting Estimate
In February 2023, we assessed the useful lives of our property, plant, and equipment.

[nvda_2024_7_P043_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P043 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Change in Accounting Estimate
Based on advances in technology and usage rate, we increased the estimated useful life of a majority of the server, storage, and network equipment from three years to a range of four to five years, and assembly and test equipment from five years to seven years.

[nvda_2024_7_P043_S003] Parent nvda_2024_7_P043 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates > Change in Accounting Estimate
The estimated effect of this change for fiscal year 2024 was a benefit of $33 million and $102 million for cost of revenue and operating expenses, respectively, which resulted in an increase in operating income of $135 million and net income of $114 million after tax, or $0.05 per both basic and diluted share.

[nvda_2024_7_P058_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P058 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Gross Profit and Gross Margin
Gross profit consists of total revenue, net of allowances, less cost of revenue.

[nvda_2024_7_P058_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P058 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Gross Profit and Gross Margin
Cost of revenue consists primarily of the cost of semiconductors, including wafer fabrication, assembly, testing and packaging, board and device costs, manufacturing support costs, including labor and overhead associated with such purchases, final test yield fallout, inventory and warranty provisions, memory and component costs, tariffs, and shipping costs.

[nvda_2024_7_P058_S003] Parent nvda_2024_7_P058 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Gross Profit and Gross Margin
Cost of revenue also includes acquisition-related costs, development costs for license and service arrangements, IP-related costs, and stock-based compensation related to personnel associated with manufacturing operations.

[nvda_2024_7_P059_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P059 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Gross Profit and Gross Margin
Our overall gross margin increased to 72.7% in fiscal year 2024 from 56.9% in fiscal year 2023.

[nvda_2024_7_P059_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P059 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Gross Profit and Gross Margin

[nvda_2024_7_P060_S003] Parent nvda_2024_7_P060 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Gross Profit and Gross Margin
The net effect on our gross margin was an unfavorable impact of 2.7% and 7.5% in fiscal years 2024 and 2023, respectively.

[nvda_2024_7_P067_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P067 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Income Taxes

[nvda_2024_7_P069_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P069 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Income Taxes

[nvda_2024_7_P069_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P069 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Income Taxes

[nvda_2024_7_P070_S001] Parent nvda_2024_7_P070 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Income Taxes

[nvda_2024_7_P070_S002] Parent nvda_2024_7_P070 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Income Taxes

[nvda_2024_7_P070_S003] Parent nvda_2024_7_P070 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Income Taxes

[nvda_2024_15_P007_S001] Parent nvda_2024_15_P007 - Item 15 - Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm > Definition and Limitations of Internal Control over Financial Reporting
A company's internal control over financial reporting is a process designed to provide reasonable assurance regarding the reliability of financial reporting and the preparation of financial statements for external purposes in accordance with generally accepted accounting principles.

[nvda_2024_15_P007_S002] Parent nvda_2024_15_P007 - Item 15 - Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm > Definition and Limitations of Internal Control over Financial Reporting
A company's internal control over financial reporting includes those policies and procedures that (i) pertain to the maintenance of records that, in reasonable detail, accurately and fairly reflect the transactions and dispositions of the assets of the company; (ii) provide reasonable assurance that transactions are recorded as necessary to permit preparation of financial statements in accordance with generally accepted accounting principles, and that receipts and expenditures of the company are being made only in accordance with authorizations of management and directors of the company; and (iii) provide reasonable assurance regarding prevention or timely detection of unauthorized acquisition, use, or disposition of the company's assets that could have a material effect on the financial statements.

[nvda_2024_15_P008_S001] Parent nvda_2024_15_P008 - Item 15 - Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm > Definition and Limitations of Internal Control over Financial Reporting
Because of its inherent limitations, internal control over financial reporting may not prevent or detect misstatements.

[nvda_2024_15_P008_S002] Parent nvda_2024_15_P008 - Item 15 - Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm > Definition and Limitations of Internal Control over Financial Reporting
Also, projections of any evaluation of effectiveness to future periods are subject to the risk that controls may become inadequate because of changes in conditions, or that the degree of compliance with the policies or procedures may deteriorate.

[nvda_2024_15_P009_S001] Parent nvda_2024_15_P009 - Item 15 - Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm > Critical Audit Matters
The critical audit matter communicated below is a matter arising from the current period audit of the consolidated financial statements that was communicated or required to be communicated to the audit committee and that (i) relates to accounts or disclosures that are material to the consolidated financial statements and (ii) involved our especially challenging, subjective, or complex judgments.

[nvda_2024_15_P009_S002] Parent nvda_2024_15_P009 - Item 15 - Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm > Critical Audit Matters
The communication of critical audit matters does not alter in any way our opinion on the consolidated financial statements, taken as a whole, and we are not, by communicating the critical audit matter below, providing a separate opinion on the critical audit matter or on the accounts or disclosures to which it relates.

[nvda_2024_15_P010_S001] Parent nvda_2024_15_P010 - Item 15 - Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm > Critical Audit Matters
Valuation of Inventories - Provisions for Excess or Obsolete Inventories and Excess Product Purchase Commitments

[nvda_2024_15_P010_S002] Parent nvda_2024_15_P010 - Item 15 - Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm > Critical Audit Matters
As described in Notes 1, 10 and 13 to the consolidated financial statements, the Company charges cost of sales for inventory provisions to write-down inventory for excess or obsolete inventory and for excess product purchase commitments.

[nvda_2024_15_P010_S003] Parent nvda_2024_15_P010 - Item 15 - Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm > Critical Audit Matters
Most of the Company's inventory provisions relate to excess quantities of products, based on the Company's inventory levels and future product purchase commitments compared to assumptions about future demand and market conditions.

[nvda_2024_15_P010_S004] Parent nvda_2024_15_P010 - Item 15 - Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm > Critical Audit Matters
As of January 28, 2024, the Company's consolidated inventories balance was $5.3 billion and the Company's consolidated outstanding inventory purchase and long-term supply and capacity obligations balance was $16.1 billion, of which a significant portion relates to inventory purchase obligations.

[nvda_2024_15_P011_S001] Parent nvda_2024_15_P011 - Item 15 - Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm > Critical Audit Matters
The principal considerations for our determination that performing procedures relating to the valuation of inventories, specifically the provisions for excess or obsolete inventories and excess product purchase commitments, is a critical audit matter are the significant judgment by management when developing provisions for excess or obsolete inventories and excess product purchase commitments, including developing assumptions related to future demand and market conditions.

[nvda_2024_15_P011_S002] Parent nvda_2024_15_P011 - Item 15 - Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm > Critical Audit Matters
This in turn led to significant auditor judgment, subjectivity, and effort in performing procedures and evaluating management's assumptions related to future demand and market conditions.

[nvda_2024_15_P012_S001] Parent nvda_2024_15_P012 - Item 15 - Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm > Critical Audit Matters
Addressing the matter involved performing procedures and evaluating audit evidence in connection with forming our overall opinion on the consolidated financial statements.

[nvda_2024_15_P012_S002] Parent nvda_2024_15_P012 - Item 15 - Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm > Critical Audit Matters
These procedures included testing the effectiveness of controls relating to management's provisions for excess or obsolete inventories and excess product purchase commitments, including controls over management's assumptions related to future demand and market conditions.

[nvda_2024_15_P012_S003] Parent nvda_2024_15_P012 - Item 15 - Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm > Critical Audit Matters
These procedures also included, among others, testing management's process for developing the provisions for excess or obsolete inventories and excess product purchase commitments; evaluating the appropriateness of management's approach; testing the completeness and accuracy of underlying data used in the approach; and evaluating the reasonableness of management's assumptions related to future demand and market conditions.

[nvda_2024_15_P012_S004] Parent nvda_2024_15_P012 - Item 15 - Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm > Critical Audit Matters
Evaluating management's assumptions related to future demand and market conditions involved evaluating whether the assumptions used by management were reasonable considering (i) current and past results, including historical product life cycle, (ii) the consistency with external market and industry data, and (iii) changes in technology.

[nvda_2024_15_P013_S001] Parent nvda_2024_15_P013 - Item 15 - Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm > Critical Audit Matters > /s/ PricewaterhouseCoopers LLP

[nvda_2024_15_P023_S001] Parent nvda_2024_15_P023 - Item 15 - Note 1 - Organization and Summary of Significant Accounting Policies > Fiscal Year
